with open('numba/stencils/stencilparfor.py') as f:
    lines = f.readlines()

# Find indices
start = None
end = None
for i, line in enumerate(lines):
    if 'Generate the code to fill just the border with zero_var' in line and start is None:
        # the line before is the "# ------------------" comment
        start = i - 1
    if start is not None and i > start and '            # ------------------' in line and 'equiv_set.insert_equiv' not in line:
        # This is the closing separator. Check next non-empty line.
        j = i + 1
        while j < len(lines) and lines[j].strip() == '':
            j += 1
        if 'equiv_set.insert_equiv' in lines[j]:
            end = i
            break

print("start line:", start+1, "end line:", end+1)
assert start is not None and end is not None

new_lines.append('            if constant_mode:\n')
for i in range(start, end + 1):
    line = lines[i]
    if line.strip() == '':
        new_lines.append('                \n')
    else:
        new_lines.append('    ' + line)
new_lines.extend(lines[end+1:])

        self.typemap[valid_var.name] = types.boolean
        new_body.append(ir.Assign(ir.Expr.getitem(bound_var, valid_const_var, loc),
                                  valid_var, loc))'''
assert old in content
content = content.replace(old, new, 1)
with open('numba/stencils/stencilparfor.py', 'w') as f:

COMMAND:
cd /app && cat <<'EOF' > /tmp/test_parallel2.py
import numpy as np
from numba import stencil, njit

def check(label, got, expected):
    ok = np.allclose(got, expected)
    print(label, "OK" if ok else "FAIL", "got=", got, "exp=", expected)
    if not ok:
        raise SystemExit(1)

# 1D wrap parallel
@stencil('wrap')
def wrap1(a):
    return a[-1] + a[0] + a[1]
a = np.arange(5, dtype=np.float64)
@njit(parallel=True)
def pw(a):
    return wrap1(a)
check("parallel 1D wrap", pw(a), [5,3,6,9,7])

# 2D wrap parallel
@stencil('wrap')
def wrap2(a):
    return a[-1, 0] + a[0, 0] + a[0, -1]
a2 = np.arange(9, dtype=np.float64).reshape(3,3)
@njit(parallel=True)
def pw2(a):
    return wrap2(a)
expected = np.zeros((3,3))
for i in range(3):
    for j in range(3):
        expected[i,j] = a2[(i-1)%3, j] + a2[i,j] + a2[i,(j-1)%3]
check("parallel 2D wrap", pw2(a2), expected)

# 2D per-dim parallel
@stencil(mode=('wrap', 'nearest'))
def wn(a):
    return a[-1, 1]
@njit(parallel=True)
def pwn(a):
    return wn(a)
expected = np.zeros((3,3))
for i in range(3):
    for j in range(3):
        expected[i,j] = a2[(i-1)%3, min(j+1,2)]
check("parallel per-dim", pwn(a2), expected)

# reflect cval fallback parallel
@stencil('reflect', cval=-77.0)
def refl_big(a):
    return a[-5]
a1 = np.arange(3, dtype=np.float64)
@njit(parallel=True)
def prb(a):
    return refl_big(a)
check("parallel reflect cval", prb(a1), [-77, 0, 1])

# parallel with out=
out = np.empty_like(a)
@njit(parallel=True)
def pwo(a, o):
    wrap1(a, out=o)
    return o
check("parallel with out", pwo(a, out), [5,3,6,9,7])

# constant mode parallel still works
@stencil
def const1(a):
    return a[-1] + a[0] + a[1]
@njit(parallel=True)
def pc(a):
    return const1(a)
check("parallel constant regression", pc(a), [0,3,6,9,0])

print("ALL PARALLEL OK")
EOF

    def test_basic96(self):
        """ 1D slice. """
        def kernel(a):
            return np.median(a[-1:2])
        # ----------------------------------------------------------------------
        # Autogenerated kernel

        def __kernel(a, neighborhood):
            self.check_stencil_arrays(a, neighborhood=neighborhood)
            __retdtype = kernel(a)
            __b0 = np.full(a.shape, 0, dtype=type(__retdtype))
            for __an in range(1, a.shape[0] - 1):
                __b0[__an,] = np.median(a[__an + -1:__an + 2])
            return __b0
        # ----------------------------------------------------------------------
        a = np.arange(20, dtype=np.uint32)
        nh = ((-1, 1),)
        expected = __kernel(a, nh)
        self.check_against_expected(kernel, expected, a,
                                    options={'neighborhood': nh})

    @unittest.skip("not yet supported")

    def test_basic98(self):
        """ Test issue #7286 where the cval is a np attr/string-based numerical
        constant"""
        for cval in (np.nan, np.inf, -np.inf, float('inf'), -float('inf')):
            def kernel(a):
                return a[0, 0]
            ## -----------------------------------------------------------------
            ## Autogenerated kernel

            def __kernel(a, neighborhood):
                self.check_stencil_arrays(a, neighborhood=neighborhood)
                __retdtype = kernel(a)
                __b0 = np.full(a.shape, cval, dtype=type(__retdtype))
                for __bn in range(1, a.shape[1] - 1):
                    for __an in range(1, a.shape[0] - 1):
                        __b0[__an, __bn] = a[__an + 0, __bn + 0]
                return __b0

            ## -----------------------------------------------------------------
            a = np.arange(6.).reshape((2, 3))
            nh = ((-1, 1), (-1, 1),)
            expected = __kernel(a, nh)
            self.check_against_expected(kernel, expected, a,
                                        options={'neighborhood': nh,
                                                 'cval':cval})

with open('numba/tests/test_stencils.py') as f:
    content = f.read()

test_code = '''

class TestStencilModes(TestStencilBase):
    """Tests for the ``mode`` parameter of ``@stencil`` for out-of-bounds
    access handling.
    """

    def check_modes(self, pyfunc, expected, *args, **options):
        """Compare a stencil decorated with ``mode``/``cval`` options against
        the expected output for direct, njit and parallel (parfor) calls.
        """
        # Direct @stencil call.
        sf = stencil(pyfunc, **options)
        np.testing.assert_almost_equal(sf(*args), expected, decimal=5)

        # njit wrapped call.
        if len(args) == 1:
            def wrap(a):
                return sf(a)
        elif len(args) == 2:
            def wrap(a, b):
                return sf(a, b)
        else:
            raise ValueError("check_modes supports at most 2 arguments")

        sig = tuple([numba.typeof(x) for x in args])
        cfunc = self.compile_njit(wrap, sig)
        np.testing.assert_almost_equal(cfunc.entry_point(*args), expected,
                                       decimal=5)

        # parallel (parfor) wrapped call.
        cpfunc = self.compile_parallel(wrap, sig)
        np.testing.assert_almost_equal(cpfunc.entry_point(*args), expected,
                                       decimal=5)

    def test_mode_wrap_1d(self):
        @stencil('wrap')
        def kernel(a):
            return a[-1] + a[0] + a[1]
        a = np.arange(5, dtype=np.float64)
        expected = np.array([5., 3., 6., 9., 7.])
        self.check_modes(kernel, expected, a)

    def test_mode_nearest_1d(self):
        @stencil('nearest')
        def kernel(a):
            return a[-1] + a[0] + a[1]
        a = np.arange(5, dtype=np.float64)
        expected = np.array([1., 3., 6., 9., 11.])
        self.check_modes(kernel, expected, a)

    def test_mode_reflect_1d(self):
        @stencil('reflect')
        def kernel(a):
            return a[-1] + a[0] + a[1]
        a = np.arange(5, dtype=np.float64)
        expected = np.array([2., 3., 6., 9., 10.])
        self.check_modes(kernel, expected, a)

    def test_mode_symmetric_1d(self):
        @stencil('symmetric')
        def kernel(a):
            return a[-1] + a[0] + a[1]
        a = np.arange(5, dtype=np.float64)
        expected = np.array([1., 3., 6., 9., 11.])
        self.check_modes(kernel, expected, a)

    def test_mode_constant_1d(self):
        @stencil('constant')
        def kernel(a):
            return a[-1] + a[0] + a[1]
        a = np.arange(5, dtype=np.float64)
        expected = np.array([0., 3., 6., 9., 0.])
        self.check_modes(kernel, expected, a)

    def test_mode_keyword(self):
        @stencil(mode='nearest')
        def kernel(a):
            return a[-1]
        a = np.arange(5, dtype=np.float64)
        expected = np.array([0., 0., 1., 2., 3.])
        self.check_modes(kernel, expected, a)

    def test_mode_2d_wrap(self):
        @stencil('wrap')
        def kernel(a):
            return a[-1, 0] + a[0, 0] + a[0, -1]
        a = np.arange(9, dtype=np.float64).reshape(3, 3)
        expected = np.zeros((3, 3))
        for i in range(3):
            for j in range(3):
                expected[i, j] = (a[(i - 1) % 3, j] + a[i, j] +
                                  a[i, (j - 1) % 3])
        self.check_modes(kernel, expected, a)

    def test_mode_per_dimension(self):
        @stencil(mode=('wrap', 'nearest'))
        def kernel(a):
            return a[-1, 1]
        a = np.arange(9, dtype=np.float64).reshape(3, 3)
        expected = np.zeros((3, 3))
        for i in range(3):
            for j in range(3):
                expected[i, j] = a[(i - 1) % 3, min(j + 1, 2)]
        self.check_modes(kernel, expected, a)

    def test_mode_per_dimension_mixed_constant(self):
        @stencil(mode=('wrap', 'constant'))
        def kernel(a):
            return a[-1, 1]
        a = np.arange(9, dtype=np.float64).reshape(3, 3)
        expected = np.zeros((3, 3))
        for i in range(3):
            for j in range(3):
                if 0 <= i - 1 < 3 and j + 1 < 3:
                    expected[i, j] = a[i - 1, j + 1]
        self.check_modes(kernel, expected, a)

    def test_mode_reflect_cval_fallback(self):
        @stencil('reflect', cval=-77.0)
        def kernel(a):
            return a[-5]
        a = np.arange(3, dtype=np.float64)
        # With a single reflection, -5 in a size-3 dimension is still out of
        # bounds so cval is used; -4 and -3 reflect to valid indices.
        expected = np.array([-77., 0., 1.])
        self.check_modes(kernel, expected, a)

    def test_mode_symmetric_cval_fallback(self):
        @stencil('symmetric', cval=-55.0)
        def kernel(a):
            return a[-7]
        a = np.arange(3, dtype=np.float64)
        expected = np.array([-55., 0., 1.])
        self.check_modes(kernel, expected, a)

    def test_mode_with_neighborhood(self):
        @stencil('wrap', neighborhood=((-1, 1),))
        def kernel(a):
            return a[-1] + a[0] + a[1]
        a = np.arange(5, dtype=np.float64)
        expected = np.array([5., 3., 6., 9., 7.])
        self.check_modes(kernel, expected, a)

    def test_mode_with_standard_indexing(self):
        @stencil('wrap', standard_indexing=('b',))
        def kernel(a, b):
            return a[-1] + b[0]
        a = np.arange(5, dtype=np.float64)
        b = np.array([100.0])
        expected = np.array([104., 100., 101., 102., 103.])
        self.check_modes(kernel, expected, a, b)

    def test_mode_with_out(self):
        @stencil('wrap')
        def kernel(a):
            return a[-1] + a[0] + a[1]
        a = np.arange(5, dtype=np.float64)
        expected = np.array([5., 3., 6., 9., 7.])
        out = np.empty_like(a)
        kernel(a, out=out)
        np.testing.assert_almost_equal(out, expected)

        # Also check parallel out.
        @numba.njit(parallel=True)
        def parallel_out(a, o):
            kernel(a, out=o)
            return o
        out2 = np.empty_like(a)
        np.testing.assert_almost_equal(parallel_out(a, out2), expected)

    def test_mode_invalid_raises(self):
        with self.assertRaises(NumbaValueError):
            @stencil('bogus')
            def kernel(a):
                return a[0]

    def test_mode_tuple_length_mismatch(self):
        @stencil(mode=('wrap', 'nearest'))
        def kernel(a):
            return a[-1]
        a = np.arange(5, dtype=np.float64)
        with self.assertRaises(NumbaValueError):
            kernel(a)

    def test_mode_cval_ignored_for_nonconstant(self):
        @stencil('nearest', cval=-99.0)
        def kernel(a):
            return a[-1] + a[0] + a[1]
        a = np.arange(5, dtype=np.float64)
        expected = np.array([1., 3., 6., 9., 11.])
        self.check_modes(kernel, expected, a)


'''

marker = 'if __name__ == "__main__":'
assert marker in content
content = content.replace(marker, test_code + marker, 1)
with open('numba/tests/test_stencils.py', 'w') as f:
    f.write(content)
print("test class added")

        """

    def test_mode_2d_wrap(self):

    def test_mode_with_neighborhood(self):
        def kernel(a):
            return a[-1] + a[0] + a[1]
        a = np.arange(5, dtype=np.float64)
        expected = np.array([5., 3., 6., 9., 7.])
        self.check_modes(kernel, expected, a, mode='wrap',
                         neighborhood=((-1, 1),))

    def test_mode_with_standard_indexing(self):
        def kernel(a, b):
            return a[-1] + b[0]
        a = np.arange(5, dtype=np.float64)
        b = np.array([100.0])
        expected = np.array([104., 100., 101., 102., 103.])
        self.check_modes(kernel, expected, a, b, mode='wrap',
                         standard_indexing=('b',))

    def test_mode_with_out(self):
        @stencil('wrap')
        def kernel(a):
            return a[-1] + a[0] + a[1]
        a = np.arange(5, dtype=np.float64)
        expected = np.array([5., 3., 6., 9., 7.])
        out = np.empty_like(a)
        kernel(a, out=out)
        np.testing.assert_almost_equal(out, expected)

        # Also check parallel out.
        @numba.njit(parallel=True)
        def parallel_out(a, o):

        out2 = np.empty_like(a)
        np.testing.assert_almost_equal(parallel_out(a, out2), expected)

    def test_mode_invalid_raises(self):
        with self.assertRaises(NumbaValueError):
            @stencil('bogus')
            def kernel(a):
                return a[0]

    def test_mode_tuple_length_mismatch(self):
        @stencil(mode=('wrap', 'nearest'))
        def kernel(a):
            return a[-1]
        a = np.arange(5, dtype=np.float64)
        with self.assertRaises(NumbaValueError):
            kernel(a)

    def test_mode_cval_ignored_for_nonconstant(self):
        def kernel(a):
            return a[-1] + a[0] + a[1]
        a = np.arange(5, dtype=np.float64)
        expected = np.array([1., 3., 6., 9., 11.])
        self.check_modes(kernel, expected, a, mode='nearest', cval=-99.0)


'''

content = content[:start_idx] + new_test + content[end_idx:]
with open('numba/tests/test_stencils.py', 'w') as f:
    f.write(content)
print("test class rewritten")

    # called on function without specifying mode style

cd /app && nl -ba docs/source/user/stencil.rst | sed -n '135,200p'
OUTPUT:
   135	
   136	Stencil decorator options
   137	=========================
   138	
   139	.. note::
   140	   The stencil decorator may be augmented in the future to provide additional
   141	   mechanisms for border handling. At present, only one behaviour is
   142	   implemented, ``"constant"`` (see ``func_or_mode`` below for details).
   143	
   144	.. _stencil-neighborhood:
   145	
   146	``neighborhood``
   147	----------------
   148	
   149	Sometimes it may be inconvenient to write the stencil kernel
   150	exclusively with ``Integer`` literals.  For example, let us say we
   151	would like to compute the trailing 30-day moving average of a
   152	time series of data.  One could write
   153	``(a[-29] + a[-28] + ... + a[-1] + a[0]) / 30`` but the stencil
   154	decorator offers a more concise form using the ``neighborhood``
   155	option::
   156	
   157	   @stencil(neighborhood = ((-29, 0),))
   158	   def kernel2(a):
   159	       cumul = 0
   160	       for i in range(-29, 1):
   161	           cumul += a[i]
   162	       return cumul / 30
   163	
   164	The neighborhood option is a tuple of tuples.  The outer tuple's
   165	length is equal to the number of dimensions of the input array.
   166	The inner tuple's lengths are always two because
   167	each element of the inner tuple corresponds to minimum and
   168	maximum index offsets used in the corresponding dimension.
   169	
   170	If a user specifies a neighborhood but the kernel accesses elements outside the
   171	specified neighborhood, **the behavior is undefined.**
   172	
   173	.. _stencil-mode:
   174	
   175	``func_or_mode``
   176	----------------
   177	
   178	The optional ``func_or_mode`` parameter controls how the border of the output array
   179	is handled.  Currently, there is only one supported value, ``"constant"``.
   180	In ``constant`` mode, the stencil kernel is not applied in cases where
   181	the kernel would access elements outside the valid range of the input
   182	array.  In such cases, those elements in the output array are assigned
   183	to a constant value, as specified by the ``cval`` parameter.
   184	
   185	``cval``
   186	--------
   187	
   188	The optional cval parameter defaults to zero but can be set to any
   189	desired value, which is then used for the border of the output array
   190	if the ``func_or_mode`` parameter is set to ``constant``.  The cval parameter is
   191	ignored in all other modes.  The type of the cval parameter must match
   192	the return type of the stencil kernel.  If the user wishes the output
   193	array to be constructed from a particular type then they should ensure
   194	that the stencil kernel returns that type.
   195	
   196	``standard_indexing``
   197	---------------------
   198	
   199	By default, all array accesses in a stencil kernel are processed as
   200	relative indices as described above.  However, sometimes it may be

old = '''.. note::
   The stencil decorator may be augmented in the future to provide additional
   mechanisms for border handling. At present, only one behaviour is
   implemented, ``"constant"`` (see ``func_or_mode`` below for details).

.. _stencil-neighborhood:

``neighborhood``
----------------

Sometimes it may be inconvenient to write the stencil kernel
exclusively with ``Integer`` literals.  For example, let us say we
would like to compute the trailing 30-day moving average of a
time series of data.  One could write
``(a[-29] + a[-28] + ... + a[-1] + a[0]) / 30`` but the stencil
decorator offers a more concise form using the ``neighborhood``
option::

   @stencil(neighborhood = ((-29, 0),))
   def kernel2(a):
       cumul = 0
       for i in range(-29, 1):
           cumul += a[i]
       return cumul / 30

The neighborhood option is a tuple of tuples.  The outer tuple's
length is equal to the number of dimensions of the input array.
The inner tuple's lengths are always two because
each element of the inner tuple corresponds to minimum and
maximum index offsets used in the corresponding dimension.

If a user specifies a neighborhood but the kernel accesses elements outside the
specified neighborhood, **the behavior is undefined.**

.. _stencil-mode:

``func_or_mode``
----------------

The optional ``func_or_mode`` parameter controls how the border of the output array
is handled.  Currently, there is only one supported value, ``"constant"``.
In ``constant`` mode, the stencil kernel is not applied in cases where
the kernel would access elements outside the valid range of the input
array.  In such cases, those elements in the output array are assigned
to a constant value, as specified by the ``cval`` parameter.

``cval``
--------

The optional cval parameter defaults to zero but can be set to any
desired value, which is then used for the border of the output array
if the ``func_or_mode`` parameter is set to ``constant``.  The cval parameter is
ignored in all other modes.  The type of the cval parameter must match
the return type of the stencil kernel.  If the user wishes the output
array to be constructed from a particular type then they should ensure
that the stencil kernel returns that type.'''

new = '''.. _stencil-neighborhood:

``neighborhood``
----------------

Sometimes it may be inconvenient to write the stencil kernel
exclusively with ``Integer`` literals.  For example, let us say we
would like to compute the trailing 30-day moving average of a
time series of data.  One could write
``(a[-29] + a[-28] + ... + a[-1] + a[0]) / 30`` but the stencil
decorator offers a more concise form using the ``neighborhood``
option::

   @stencil(neighborhood = ((-29, 0),))
   def kernel2(a):
       cumul = 0
       for i in range(-29, 1):
           cumul += a[i]
       return cumul / 30

The neighborhood option is a tuple of tuples.  The outer tuple's
length is equal to the number of dimensions of the input array.
The inner tuple's lengths are always two because
each element of the inner tuple corresponds to minimum and
maximum index offsets used in the corresponding dimension.

If a user specifies a neighborhood but the kernel accesses elements outside the
specified neighborhood, **the behavior is undefined.**

.. _stencil-mode:

``func_or_mode`` / ``mode``
---------------------------

The optional ``func_or_mode`` parameter (also available through the
``mode`` keyword option) controls how out-of-bounds accesses are handled
when applying the stencil kernel.  A single string applies the same mode
to every dimension::

   @stencil('wrap')
   def kernel1(a):
       return a[-1] + a[0] + a[1]

A tuple of strings may be supplied with the ``mode`` keyword for
per-dimension control; its length must match the number of dimensions of
the input array::

   @stencil(mode=('wrap', 'nearest'))
   def kernel2(a):
       return a[-1, 1]

The supported modes are:

* ``"constant"`` (default): the stencil kernel is not applied in cases
  where the kernel would access elements outside the valid range of the
  input array.  In such cases, those elements in the output array are
  assigned to a constant value, as specified by the ``cval`` parameter.
* ``"wrap"``: circular indexing, where out-of-bounds indices wrap around
  to the other side of the array.
* ``"nearest"``: out-of-bounds indices are clamped to the nearest edge of
  the array.
* ``"reflect"``: mirror the array at the edges without repeating the edge
  values.
* ``"symmetric"``: mirror the array at the edges with the edge values
  repeated.

For the ``reflect`` and ``symmetric`` modes, if the reflected index is
still out of bounds after a single reflection, the ``cval`` value is used
for that access.

``cval``
--------

The optional cval parameter defaults to zero but can be set to any
desired value, which is then used for the border of the output array
if the mode is set to ``constant``.  It is also used for ``reflect`` and
``symmetric`` accesses whose reflected index is still out of bounds.
The cval parameter is ignored in all other modes.  The type of the cval
parameter must match the return type of the stencil kernel.  If the user
wishes the output array to be constructed from a particular type then
they should ensure that the stencil kernel returns that type.'''

assert old in content
content = content.replace(old, new, 1)
with open('docs/source/user/stencil.rst', 'w') as f:
    f.write(content)
print("docs updated")

cd /app && cat > docs/upcoming_changes/0000.new_feature.rst <<'EOF'
Add a ``mode`` parameter to ``@stencil`` for handling out-of-bounds accesses.
The supported modes are ``wrap`` (circular), ``nearest`` (clamp to edge),
``reflect`` (mirror without repeating edge), ``symmetric`` (mirror with
repeating edge) and ``constant`` (default, boundary positions set to
``cval``).  A single mode string may be passed positionally, e.g.
``@stencil('wrap')``, and per-dimension control is available via
``mode=('wrap', 'nearest')``.  For ``reflect`` and ``symmetric``, accesses
whose reflected index is still out of bounds use ``cval``.
EOF

COMMAND:
cd /app && cat <<'EOF' > /tmp/test_edge.py
import numpy as np
from numba import stencil, njit

def show(label, fn, *args):
    try:
        print(label, "=>", fn(*args))
    except Exception as e:
        import traceback
        print(label, "=> ERROR:", type(e).__name__, str(e)[:300])
        traceback.print_exc()

# size-1 arrays
a1 = np.array([10.0])
@stencil('wrap')
def w1(a):
    return a[-1] + a[0]
show("wrap size1", w1, a1)

@stencil('nearest')
def n1(a):
    return a[-1] + a[0]
show("nearest size1", n1, a1)

@stencil('reflect')
def r1(a):
    return a[-1]
show("reflect size1", r1, a1)  # expect cval=0 since -1 reflects out of bounds for n=1

@stencil('symmetric')
def s1(a):
    return a[-1]
show("symmetric size1", s1, a1)  # expect a[0]=10

# variable neighborhood with mode
@stencil('wrap', neighborhood=((w,),))
def vn(a, w):
    return a[-w] + a[w]
a = np.arange(5, dtype=np.float64)
show("variable nh wrap", vn, a, 1)  # wrap: a[-1]+a[1]; i=0:4+1=5, i=4:3+0=3

# multiple arrays with mode
@stencil('wrap')
def multi(a, b):
    return a[-1] + b[1]
b = np.arange(5, dtype=np.float64)
show("multi wrap", multi, a, b)
# i=0: a[-1]=4 + b[1]=1 = 5; i=4: a[3]=3+b[0]=0 = 3

# cval type promotion: int array, float cval for reflect fallback
@stencil('reflect', cval=0.5)
def rp(a):
    return a[-5]
ai = np.arange(3, dtype=np.int64)
show("reflect cval float on int array", rp, ai)
# n=3: i=0 idx=-5 invalid -> cval 0.5; i=1 idx=-4 -> a[0]=0; i=2 idx=-3 -> a[1]=1
# output should be float [0.5, 0, 1]

# wrap inside njit parallel with multiple arrays
@njit(parallel=True)
def p_multi(a, b):
    return multi(a, b)
show("multi wrap parallel", p_multi, a, b)

# 3D wrap
a3 = np.arange(8, dtype=np.float64).reshape(2,2,2)
@stencil('wrap')
def w3(a):
    return a[-1, 0, 0] + a[0, 0, 0]
show("3D wrap", w3, a3)

# 3D parallel wrap
@njit(parallel=True)
def p3(a):
    return w3(a)
show("3D wrap parallel", p3, a3)
EOF

show("reflect cval float on int array", rp, ai)

# wrap inside njit parallel with multiple arrays
@njit(parallel=True)
def p_multi(a, b):
    return multi(a, b)
show("multi wrap parallel", p_multi, a, b)

# 3D wrap
a3 = np.arange(8, dtype=np.float64).reshape(2,2,2)
@stencil('wrap')
def w3(a):
    return a[-1, 0, 0] + a[0, 0, 0]
show("3D wrap", w3, a3)

@njit(parallel=True)
def p3(a):
    return w3(a)
show("3D wrap parallel", p3, a3)

import numpy as np
from numba import stencil, njit

def check(label, got, expected):
    ok = np.allclose(got, expected)
    print(label, "OK" if ok else "FAIL", got, expected)
    if not ok:
        raise SystemExit(1)

a2 = np.arange(9, dtype=np.float64).reshape(3,3)

# constant first dim, nearest second
@stencil(mode=('constant', 'nearest'))
def cn(a):
    return a[-1, 1]
expected = np.zeros((3,3))
for i in range(3):
    for j in range(3):
        if 0 <= i-1 < 3:
            expected[i,j] = a[i-1, min(j+1,2)]
check("('constant','nearest')", cn(a2), expected)

# all options together: mode, cval, neighborhood, standard_indexing
@stencil(mode='wrap', cval=-1.0, neighborhood=((-1,1),), standard_indexing=('b',))
def allopts(a, b):
    return a[-1] + b[0]
a = np.arange(5, dtype=np.float64)
b = np.array([100.0])
check("all opts", allopts(a, b), [104,100,101,102,103])

# all options + parallel
@njit(parallel=True)
def pallopts(a, b):
    return allopts(a, b)
check("all opts parallel", pallopts(a, b), [104,100,101,102,103])

# mode with out and cval reflect fallback
@stencil('reflect', cval=-5.0)
def rbig(a):
    return a[-5]
a1 = np.arange(3, dtype=np.float64)
out = np.full(3, 999.0)
rbig(a1, out=out)
check("reflect out cval", out, [-5, 0, 1])

# mode in a lambda via stencil call inside njit
@njit(parallel=True)
def lambda_stencil(a):
    s = stencil(lambda x: x[-1] + x[0], mode='wrap')
    return s(a)
check("lambda stencil mode parallel", lambda_stencil(a), [5,3,6,9,7])

print("ALL COMBOS OK")

import numpy as np
from numba import stencil, njit

a = np.arange(5, dtype=np.float64)

# existing pattern without mode (inside njit parallel)
@njit(parallel=True)
def f1(a):
    B = np.zeros_like(a)
    stencil(lambda x: x[-1] + x[0] + x[1])(a, out=B)
    return B
print("f1 (no mode):", f1(a))

# with mode keyword
@njit(parallel=True)
def f2(a):
    B = np.zeros_like(a)
    stencil(lambda x: x[-1] + x[0] + x[1], mode='wrap')(a, out=B)
    return B
print("f2 (mode=wrap):", f2(a))

   130	            cfg = compute_cfg_from_blocks(self.func_ir.blocks)
   131	            debug_print("start inline arraycall")
   132	            _debug_dump(cfg)
   133	            loops = cfg.loops()
   134	            sized_loops = [(k, len(loops[k].body)) for k in loops.keys()]
   135	            visited = []
   136	            # We go over all loops, bigger loops first (outer first)
   137	            for k, s in sorted(sized_loops, key=lambda tup: tup[1],
   138	                               reverse=True):
   139	                visited.append(k)
   140	                if guard(_inline_arraycall, self.func_ir, cfg, visited,
   141	                         loops[k], self.swapped,
   142	                         self.parallel_options.comprehension, self.typed):
   143	                    modified = True
   144	            if modified:
   145	                _fix_nested_array(self.func_ir)
   146	
   147	        if modified:
   148	            # clean up now dead/unreachable blocks, e.g. unconditionally raising
   149	            # an exception in an inlined function would render some parts of the
   150	            # inliner unreachable
   151	            cfg = compute_cfg_from_blocks(self.func_ir.blocks)
   152	            for dead in cfg.dead_nodes():
   153	                del self.func_ir.blocks[dead]
   154	
   155	            # run dead code elimination
   156	            dead_code_elimination(self.func_ir)
   157	            # do label renaming
   158	            self.func_ir.blocks = rename_labels(self.func_ir.blocks)
   159	
   160	        # inlining done, strip dels
   161	        remove_dels(self.func_ir.blocks)
   162	
   163	        debug_print("END")
   164	
   165	    def _inline_reduction(self, work_list, block, i, expr, call_name):
   166	        # only inline reduction in sequential execution, parallel handling
   167	        # is done in ParforPass.
   168	        require(not self.parallel_options.reduction)
   169	        require(call_name == ('reduce', 'builtins') or
   170	                call_name == ('reduce', '_functools'))
   171	        if len(expr.args) not in (2, 3):
   172	            raise TypeError("invalid reduce call, "
   173	                            "two arguments are required (optional initial "
   174	                            "value can also be specified)")
   175	        check_reduce_func(self.func_ir, expr.args[0])
   176	
   177	        def reduce_func(f, A, v=None):
   178	            it = iter(A)
   179	            if v is not None:
   180	                s = v
   181	            else:
   182	                s = next(it)
   183	            for a in it:
   184	                s = f(s, a)
   185	            return s
   186	
   187	        inline_closure_call(
   188	            self.func_ir, self.func_ir.func_id.func.__globals__,
   189	            block, i, reduce_func, work_list=work_list,
   190	            callee_validator=callee_ir_validator
   191	        )
   192	        return True
   193	
   194	    def _inline_stencil(self, instr, call_name, func_def):
   195	        from numba.stencils.stencil import StencilFunc
   196	        lhs = instr.target
   197	        expr = instr.value
   198	        # We keep the escaping variables of the stencil kernel
   199	        # alive by adding them to the actual kernel call as extra
   200	        # keyword arguments, which is ignored anyway.
   201	        if (isinstance(func_def, ir.Global) and
   202	                func_def.name == 'stencil' and
   203	                isinstance(func_def.value, StencilFunc)):
   204	            if expr.kws:
   205	                expr.kws += func_def.value.kws
   206	            else:
   207	                expr.kws = func_def.value.kws
   208	            return True
   209	        # Otherwise we proceed to check if it is a call to numba.stencil
   210	        require(call_name == ('stencil', 'numba.stencils.stencil') or
   211	                call_name == ('stencil', 'numba'))
   212	        require(expr not in self._processed_stencils)
   213	        self._processed_stencils.append(expr)
   214	        if not len(expr.args) == 1:
   215	            raise ValueError("As a minimum Stencil requires"
   216	                             " a kernel as an argument")
   217	        stencil_def = guard(get_definition, self.func_ir, expr.args[0])
   218	        require(isinstance(stencil_def, ir.Expr) and
   219	                stencil_def.op == "make_function")
   220	        kernel_ir = get_ir_of_code(self.func_ir.func_id.func.__globals__,
   221	                                   stencil_def.code)
   222	        options = dict(expr.kws)
   223	        if 'neighborhood' in options:
   224	            fixed = guard(self._fix_stencil_neighborhood, options)
   225	            if not fixed:
   226	                raise ValueError(
   227	                    "stencil neighborhood option should be a tuple"
   228	                    " with constant structure such as ((-w, w),)"
   229	                )
   230	        if 'index_offsets' in options:
   231	            fixed = guard(self._fix_stencil_index_offsets, options)
   232	            if not fixed:
   233	                raise ValueError(
   234	                    "stencil index_offsets option should be a tuple"
   235	                    " with constant structure such as (offset, )"
   236	                )
   237	        sf = StencilFunc(kernel_ir, 'constant', options)
   238	        sf.kws = expr.kws # hack to keep variables live
   239	        sf_global = ir.Global('stencil', sf, expr.loc)
   240	        self.func_ir._definitions[lhs.name] = [sf_global]
   241	        instr.value = sf_global
   242	        return True
   243	
   244	    def _fix_stencil_neighborhood(self, options):
   245	        """
   246	        Extract the two-level tuple representing the stencil neighborhood
   247	        from the program IR to provide a tuple to StencilFunc.
   248	        """
   249	        # build_tuple node with neighborhood for each dimension
   250	        dims_build_tuple = get_definition(self.func_ir, options['neighborhood'])
   251	        require(hasattr(dims_build_tuple, 'items'))
   252	        res = []
   253	        for window_var in dims_build_tuple.items:
   254	            win_build_tuple = get_definition(self.func_ir, window_var)
   255	            require(hasattr(win_build_tuple, 'items'))
   256	            res.append(tuple(win_build_tuple.items))
   257	        options['neighborhood'] = tuple(res)
   258	        return True
   259	
   260	    def _fix_stencil_index_offsets(self, options):
   261	        """
   262	        Extract the tuple representing the stencil index offsets
   263	        from the program IR to provide to StencilFunc.
   264	        """
   265	        offset_tuple = get_definition(self.func_ir, options['index_offsets'])
   266	        require(hasattr(offset_tuple, 'items'))
   267	        options['index_offsets'] = tuple(offset_tuple.items)
   268	        return True
   269	
   270	    def _inline_closure(self, work_list, block, i, func_def):

        """Extract the stencil mode option from the program IR.  The mode can
        be a single string constant or a tuple of string constants (one per
        dimension).

        mode_def = get_definition(self.func_ir, options[\u0027mode\u0027])
        if isinstance(mode_def, ir.Const) and isinstance(mode_def.value, str):

                    res.append(item_def.value)
                else:
                    return False
            options[\u0027mode\u0027] = tuple(res)
            return True
        return False

import numpy as np
from numba import stencil

@stencil('wrap', cval=-99.0)
def k(a):
    return a[-1] + a[0] + a[1]
a = np.arange(5, dtype=np.float64)
print(k(a))  # wrap ignores cval -> [5,3,6,9,7]

def k2(a):
    return a[-1] + a[0] + a[1]
print(k2(a))  # constant -> [5,3,6,9,5]

@stencil(mode=('wrap', 'nearest'), cval=7.0)
def k3(a):
    return a[-1, 1]
a2 = np.arange(9, dtype=np.float64).reshape(3,3)
print(k3(a2))

COMMAND:
cd /app && python - <<'EOF'
import numpy as np
from numba import stencil

# reflect with np.nan cval
@stencil('reflect', cval=np.nan)
def rn(a):
    return a[-5]
a1 = np.arange(3, dtype=np.float64)
r = rn(a1)
print("reflect nan:", r)
assert np.isnan(r[0]) and r[1]==0 and r[2]==1

# reflect with np.inf cval
@stencil('reflect', cval=np.inf)
def ri(a):
    return a[-5]
r = ri(a1)
print("reflect inf:", r)
assert r[0]==np.inf

# symmetric with -np.inf cval
@stencil('symmetric', cval=-np.inf)
def rni(a):
    return a[-7]
r = rni(a1)
print("symmetric -inf:", r)
assert r[0]==-np.inf

# cval as int with float array
@stencil('reflect', cval=5)
def ri2(a):
    return a[-5]
r = ri2(a1)
print("reflect int cval on float:", r)
assert r[0]==5.0

print("ALL EDGE OK")
EOF

reflect inf: [inf  0.  1.]
symmetric -inf: [-inf   0.   1.]
reflect int cval on float: [5. 0. 1.]

+    def _fix_stencil_mode(self, options):
+        """Extract the stencil mode option from the program IR.  The mode can
+        be a single string constant or a tuple of string constants (one per
+        dimension).
+        """
+        mode_def = get_definition(self.func_ir, options['mode'])
+        if isinstance(mode_def, ir.Const) and isinstance(mode_def.value, str):
+            options['mode'] = mode_def.value
+            return True
+        if hasattr(mode_def, 'items'):
+            res = []
+            for item_var in mode_def.items:
+                item_def = get_definition(self.func_ir, item_var)
+                if isinstance(item_def, ir.Const) and isinstance(item_def.value, str):
+                    res.append(item_def.value)
+                else:
+                    return False
+            options['mode'] = tuple(res)
+            return True
+        return False

cd /app && cat <<'EOF' > /tmp/test_var_nh.py
import numpy as np
from numba import stencil, njit

@stencil('wrap', neighborhood=((w,),))
def k(a, w):
    return a[-w] + a[w]

a = np.arange(5, dtype=np.float64)

# direct call
print("direct:", k(a, 1))  # wrap: [5,2,4,6,3]
print("direct w=2:", k(a, 2))  # wrap: i0: a[-2]=3+a[2]=2=5; i4: a[2]=2+a[-2]=3=5 => [5,5,5,5,5]

# njit serial
@njit
def f_serial(a, w):
    return k(a, w)
print("njit:", f_serial(a, 1))

# njit parallel
@njit(parallel=True)
def f_par(a, w):
    return k(a, w)
print("parallel:", f_par(a, 1))
print("parallel w=2:", f_par(a, 2))
EOF

STEP 198 CALL 1 RETURN 128
COMMAND:
cd /app && git commit -m "Add mode parameter to @stencil for out-of-bounds access handling

Add support for border-handling modes in the @stencil decorator:
- wrap (circular indexing)
- nearest (clamp to edge)
- reflect (mirror without repeating edge)
- symmetric (mirror with repeating edge)
- constant (default, boundary set to cval, kernel not applied)

A single mode can be specified positionally, e.g. @stencil('wrap'),
and per-dimension control is available via mode=('wrap', 'nearest').
For reflect and symmetric modes, accesses whose reflected index is
still out of bounds use cval.

The mode parameter works alongside the existing cval, neighborhood and
standard_indexing options, and is supported in both the serial and
parallel (parfor) execution paths." && git log --oneline -3

cd /app && git status && echo "=====" && python - <<'EOF'
import numpy as np
from numba import stencil, njit

@stencil('wrap')
def w(a):
    return a[-1] + a[0] + a[1]
a = np.arange(5, dtype=np.float64)
assert np.allclose(w(a), [5,3,6,9,7])

@stencil('reflect', cval=-77.0)
def r(a):
    return a[-5]
a1 = np.arange(3, dtype=np.float64)
assert np.allclose(r(a1), [-77,0,1])

@stencil(mode=('wrap','nearest'))
def wn(a):
    return a[-1,1]
a2 = np.arange(9, dtype=np.float64).reshape(3,3)
expected = np.zeros((3,3))
for i in range(3):
    for j in range(3):
        expected[i,j] = a2[(i-1)%3, min(j+1,2)]
assert np.allclose(wn(a2), expected)

@njit(parallel=True)
def p(a):
    return w(a)
assert np.allclose(p(a), [5,3,6,9,7])

try:
    @stencil('bogus')
    def bad(a):
        return a[0]
    raise AssertionError("should have raised")
except Exception as e:
    assert type(e).__name__ == 'NumbaValueError'

print("FINAL VERIFICATION PASSED")
EOF
